Chapter 20: Troubleshooting & Debugging — Fixing Helm When Things Go Wrong

Even the best charts break. Template errors, failed installs, hook timeouts, dependency conflicts, and release drift — these are the daily challenges of Helm at scale. This chapter is your debugging field manual: systematic approaches to diagnose and fix every common Helm problem, plus the tools and commands that save hours of frustration.

🔴 Advanced ⏱️ ~2 hours 🔧 Debugging 🛠️ Essential

🎯 What You'll Learn

  • ✅ Diagnose and fix common Helm errors (nil pointer, YAML parse, API mismatches)
  • ✅ Debug failed installations — reading error output, checking pod logs
  • ✅ Resolve template rendering issues with systematic debugging
  • ✅ Fix dependency resolution problems and version conflicts
  • ✅ Debug hook failures and timeouts
  • ✅ Detect and resolve release drift between Helm state and live cluster
  • ✅ Master Helm debugging tools: helm debug, helm get, helm diff

20.1 Common Helm Errors & Solutions

Top 10 Helm Errors — Quick Reference
  ERROR                                            CAUSE / SOLUTION
  ───────────────────────────────────────────────  ────────────────────────────
  "nil pointer evaluating interface {}"            Referenced a missing .Values key
                                                   → Use {{ .Values.x | default "safe" }}

  "error converting YAML to JSON"                  Template output is not valid YAML
                                                   → Check indentation, use --dry-run

  "no matches for kind Deployment"                Wrong K8s API version
                                                   → Check .Capabilities.APIVersions

  "Secret sh.helm.release.v1... already exists"    Release name conflict
                                                   → helm list, use different name

  "Error: UPGRADE FAILED"                          Resources couldn't be updated
                                                   → helm rollback, check pod logs

  "timed out waiting for the condition"            Pods didn't become ready
                                                   → kubectl describe pod, check events

  "could not find tiller"                          Using Helm 2 commands on Helm 3
                                                   → Tiller doesn't exist in Helm 3!

  "Error: unknown flag: --name"                    Helm 2 flag syntax
                                                   → In Helm 3, name is positional

  "Error: repo ... not found"                      Repository not added locally
                                                   → helm repo add, helm repo update

  "Error: no available release name found"         All generated names are taken
                                                   → Specify a name explicitly
🔴
#1: Nil Pointer Panics

Symptom: nil pointer evaluating interface {}.Values.xxx. Fix: Always use default: {{ .Values.missingKey | default "fallback" }}. For required values: {{ required "msg" .Values.key }}. This is the single most common template error.

🔴
#2: YAML Parse Errors

Symptom: error converting YAML to JSON. Fix: Run helm template --debug to see rendered YAML. Check for: improper indentation after include, missing | quote for strings with colons, incorrect nindent values.

🔴
#3: API Version Mismatches

Symptom: no matches for kind.... Fix: Use .Capabilities.APIVersions.Has to conditionally select API versions. Run helm install --dry-run to validate against the live cluster before actual deployment.

🔴
#4: Failed Upgrades

Symptom: Release stuck in failed state. Fix: helm rollback <release> to last working version. Then debug: check pod logs, events, resource quotas. Use --atomic next time for auto-rollback.

🔴
#5: ImagePullBackOff

Symptom: Pods stuck in ImagePullBackOff or ErrImagePull. Fix: Verify image.repository and image.tag in values.yaml. Check registry authentication. Test: docker pull <image>. For private registries, ensure imagePullSecrets is configured in the chart.

🔴
#6: ConfigMap/Secret Not Found

Symptom: configmap "xxx" not found or secret "xxx" not found. Fix: Check name consistency between Deployment's configMapRef/secretRef and the actual resource name. Use {{ include "fullname" . }} for consistent naming. Helm install order may matter — ConfigMaps should exist before Deployments reference them.

Error Severity Classification — What to Fix First
  🚨 CRITICAL — BLOCKS INSTALL                            🟡 WARNING — INSTALLS BUT BROKEN
  ─────────────────────────────────────                   ───────────────────────────────────
  • nil pointer evaluating interface {}                   • ImagePullBackOff (wrong tag)
  • error converting YAML to JSON                         • CrashLoopBackOff (app error)
  • no matches for kind Deployment                        • ConfigMap/Secret naming mismatch
  • Secret size exceeds 1MB                                • Resource quota exceeded
                                                           • Liveness probe failing
  ⚡ FIX IMMEDIATELY                                       🔧 FIX AFTER DEPLOY
                                                           
  🔑 RULE: Fix CRITICAL errors first — the release
     won't proceed until these are resolved.
     WARNINGs mean the release deployed but pods
     aren't healthy — check Kubernetes resources.

20.2 Debugging Failed Installations

1
Read the Error Message

Helm's error output tells you exactly what failed. helm install test ./chart --dry-run --debug provides verbose output. Look for: template name, line number, the YAML that failed, and the K8s API response.

2
Check the Release Status

helm status <release> -n <ns> shows current state and error details. helm history <release> shows the sequence of events leading to failure.

3
Inspect the Rendered Manifest

helm get manifest <release> --revision <N> shows what Helm sent to K8s. Look for: invalid YAML, missing required fields, wrong API versions, incorrect selectors.

4
Check Kubernetes Resources

kubectl get events -n <ns> — K8s events often contain the real reason. kubectl describe pod <name> — Check for image pull errors, resource constraints, volume mount issues.

5
Check Pod Logs

kubectl logs deploy/<name> -n <ns> — If pods created but crashing, logs reveal why. For hook Jobs: kubectl logs job/<hook-name>.

💡 Real Debugging Example: anihpj Failed Install

A typical anihpj deploy fails with Error: INSTALLATION FAILED. Here's the systematic fix: (1) helm status anihpj-dev -n dev shows "pods not ready". (2) kubectl get pods -n dev shows CrashLoopBackOff. (3) kubectl logs deploy/anihpj-dev -n dev reveals django.db.utils.OperationalError: could not connect to server. (4) Root cause: PostgreSQL dependency wasn't deployed because postgresql.enabled: false in values-dev.yaml. (5) Fix: Set postgresql.enabled: true and run helm dependency update && helm upgrade anihpj-dev ./chart -f values-dev.yaml -n dev --atomic. This real-world example shows why checking pod logs (not just Helm output) is essential!

20.3 Template Rendering Issues

Template Debugging Workflow
# Step 1: Lint first (catches 80% of errors)
helm lint ./chart --strict

# Step 2: Render locally with debug
helm template test ./chart -f values.yaml --debug 2>&1 | less

# Step 3: Check for common template issues
# - Missing whitespace control ({{- -}})
# - Forgotten | quote for strings with special chars
# - Wrong nindent value after include
# - Missing . (dot/scope) in nested blocks

# Step 4: Validate with kubeconform (if available)
helm template test ./chart | kubeconform -strict

# Step 5: Render with different values to isolate
helm template test ./chart -f values-prod.yaml --debug

20.4 Dependency Resolution Problems

ProblemSymptomSolution
Missing dependencyError: found in Chart.yaml, but missing in charts/Run helm dependency update
Version conflictno chart version found for xxx-1.2.3Check version constraint syntax: ~12.x vs 12.x.x
Repo not foundError: repo example not foundhelm repo add <name> <url>; helm repo update
Stale lock fileWrong version downloadedDelete Chart.lock and run helm dependency update
Conditional not workingDependency always installsEnsure condition: value is boolean false, not string "false"

20.5 Hook Failures & Timeouts

Hooks are Jobs that run synchronously — if they hang or fail, the entire release operation blocks.

⏱️
Hook Timeout

Symptom: Release stuck in pending-upgrade for 5+ minutes. Fix: Increase timeout: --timeout 10m. Check if the hook Job is actually running: kubectl get jobs -l helm.sh/hook. View logs: kubectl logs job/<hook>.

❌
Hook Failure

Symptom: Release enters failed state. Fix: Rollback (helm rollback), then debug the hook Job. Common causes: wrong image, missing env vars, DB unreachable, insufficient RBAC for the hook's ServiceAccount.

🪝
Hook Never Runs

Symptom: Hook Job created but stays pending. Fix: Check helm.sh/hook annotation is correct (exact spelling matters). Verify hook resource type (Job, Pod, ConfigMap). Check hook weight ordering — earlier hooks might be blocking.

🧹
Lingering Hook Pods

Symptom: Dozens of completed hook pods in namespace. Fix: Set "helm.sh/hook-delete-policy": hook-succeeded on your hook resources. Clean up old ones: kubectl delete pods -l helm.sh/hook.

20.6 Diagnosing Release Drift

Release drift happens when someone modifies a Kubernetes resource directly (via kubectl) instead of through Helm. This creates a difference between what Helm deployed and what's running live.

Detecting and Fixing Drift
# Detect drift: Compare Helm manifest vs live state
helm get manifest anihpj-prod -n production | kubectl diff -f -

# Using helm-diff plugin (cleaner output)
helm plugin install https://github.com/databus/helm-diff
helm diff upgrade anihpj-prod ./anihpj-chart -f values-prod.yaml

# Fix drift: Re-apply the chart (overwrites manual changes)
helm upgrade anihpj-prod ./anihpj-chart -f values-prod.yaml --reset-values

# Prevent drift: Use GitOps (Argo CD with selfHeal: true)
# Argo CD continuously reconciles and reverts manual changes

20.7 Helm Debugging Tools — helm debug, helm get, helm diff

ToolCommandWhat It ShowsWhen to Use
helm linthelm lint ./chart --strictChart.yaml syntax, template errorsFirst step — catch 80% of issues
helm templatehelm template test ./chart --debugRendered YAML without installingDebug template logic locally
--dry-runhelm install test ./chart --dry-run --debugWhat would be sent to K8s APIValidate against live cluster
helm get manifesthelm get manifest <release>Stored manifest from last deployCompare with live state
helm get valueshelm get values <release> --allAll computed valuesDebug configuration issues
helm historyhelm history <release>All revisions and statusesUnderstand what changed when
helm statushelm status <release> --show-resourcesCurrent state + resource listQuick health check
helm diff (plugin)helm diff upgrade <release> ./chartDiff of what would changeBefore upgrading in production
Debugging Decision Tree — Which Tool First?
  Something's wrong with my Helm deployment...
  │
  ├── Chart won't even lint?
  │   └──▶ helm lint ./chart --strict
  │       → Fix Chart.yaml syntax, template errors
  │
  ├── Template rendering gives errors?
  │   └──▶ helm template test ./chart --debug
  │       → Look for nil pointers, YAML parse errors, scope issues
  │
  ├── Install/upgrade failed?
  │   └──▶ helm status <release> --show-resources
  │       → helm get manifest <release>
  │       → kubectl get events -n <ns>
  │       → kubectl describe pod <name>
  │
  ├── Release is deployed but app doesn't work?
  │   └──▶ kubectl logs deploy/<app> -n <ns>
  │       → helm test <release> --logs
  │       → Check ConfigMap/Secret values
  │
  └── Unsure what changed?
      └──▶ helm diff upgrade <release> ./chart
          → Shows exactly what would be modified
Q1
What causes "nil pointer evaluating interface {}" and how do you fix it?
Answer & Explanation
Answer

Referencing a .Values key that doesn't exist. Fix: use {{ .Values.key | default "fallback" }} or {{ required "msg" .Values.key }} for mandatory values. Always guard nullable values with default or if checks.

Explanation

Go templates return nil when accessing non-existent map keys. The Sprig default function catches nil/empty values and substitutes a safe fallback. This is the #1 template error — memorize the pattern: .Values.anything | default "safe".

Explanation

"nil pointer evaluating interface" occurs when accessing nested .Values keys where an intermediate key is nil/missing. Fix: {{ .Values.key.subkey | default "fallback" }} or guard with if.

Q2
Your helm install is stuck at "pending-install" for 10 minutes. What do you do?
Answer & Explanation
Answer

(1) Check hook Jobs: kubectl get jobs -l helm.sh/hook -n <ns>. (2) View hook logs: kubectl logs job/<name>. (3) Increase timeout: --timeout 15m. (4) Check if resources are being created: kubectl get all -n <ns>. (5) If hung hook, delete the Job — Helm will see it as failure and you can rollback.

Explanation

helm install creates a new release tracked in Kubernetes Secrets. Key flags: --atomic (auto-rollback), --wait (wait for readiness), --dry-run (validate first). Always dry-run before deploying on the exam.

Q3
How do you find out EXACTLY what Helm sent to Kubernetes for a release?
Answer & Explanation
Answer

helm get manifest <release> -n <ns> — shows the complete rendered YAML that was applied. For a specific revision: helm get manifest <release> --revision <N>.

Explanation

This is a key Helm certification concept. The performance-based exam tests practical application — understanding why is as important as knowing how. Review the related chapter section for deeper context, diagrams, and hands-on practice drills.

Q4
What is release drift and how do you detect it?
Answer & Explanation
Answer

Someone modified K8s resources directly with kubectl instead of through Helm. Detect with: helm get manifest <release> | kubectl diff -f - or helm diff upgrade <release> ./chart (helm-diff plugin). Fix by re-running helm upgrade to reconcile.

Explanation

This is a key Helm certification concept. The performance-based exam tests practical application — understanding why is as important as knowing how. Review the related chapter section for deeper context, diagrams, and hands-on practice drills.

Q5
What's the first debugging command you should run when a chart won't install?
Answer & Explanation
Answer

helm lint ./chart --strict. Catches 80% of issues (syntax errors, missing required fields, deprecated APIs) instantly without touching the cluster. Then: helm template --debug → helm install --dry-run --debug. 

# Debugging Pyramid (fastest → most thorough):
helm lint ./chart --strict           # 1s  - syntax/structural errors
helm template ./chart --debug        # 2s  - rendering errors
helm install test ./chart --dry-run --debug  # 3s - API validation
helm install prod ./chart -f values.yaml --atomic --wait  # REAL

# Always run lint first on the exam!

Explanation

This is a key Helm certification concept. The performance-based exam tests practical application — understanding why is as important as knowing how. Review the related chapter section for deeper context, diagrams, and hands-on practice drills.

Q6
How do you debug a hook Job that never completes?
Answer & Explanation
Answer

kubectl get jobs -l helm.sh/hook -n <ns> to find the hook. kubectl describe job/<name> for events. kubectl logs job/<name> for output. Common causes: wrong image, DB unreachable, missing env vars from ConfigMap/Secret.

Explanation

Hooks run at lifecycle events via "helm.sh/hook" annotation. Types: pre/post-install/upgrade/delete/rollback, test. Synchronous — block the release. Use weights for ordering and delete policies for cleanup.

Q7
Chart.lock is stale — what's the fix?
Answer & Explanation
Answer

Delete Chart.lock and run helm dependency update. This re-resolves all dependency versions and regenerates the lock file with fresh digests. Then commit the new Chart.lock to Git.

Explanation

Chart.lock pins exact dependency versions and digests. Always commit to Git for reproducible builds. Rebuild with helm dependency build (no network, faster, reads lock file).

Q8
Release is in "failed" state. What are your options?
Answer & Explanation
Answer

(1) helm rollback <release> — go to last working version. (2) helm uninstall <release> --keep-history then re-install. (3) Debug and fix the cause, then helm upgrade. Use --atomic next time for auto-rollback on failure.

Explanation

This is a key Helm certification concept. The performance-based exam tests practical application — understanding why is as important as knowing how. Review the related chapter section for deeper context, diagrams, and hands-on practice drills.

Q9
What does helm template --debug do vs --dry-run?
Answer & Explanation
Answer

helm template renders YAML locally (no cluster). --dry-run connects to K8s API to validate API versions and admission webhooks. Template = offline, Dry-run = online validation. Both show rendered output.

Explanation

helm template renders YAML locally without a cluster. Unlike --dry-run, it skips API validation. Use --debug for detailed output. Ideal for CI/CD and offline development.

Q10
How can you see what values Helm actually computed for a release?
Answer & Explanation
Answer

helm get values <release> shows user-supplied values. helm get values <release> --all shows all values merged with chart defaults. This reveals exactly what configuration was applied — essential for debugging "wrong config" issues.

Explanation

This is a key Helm certification concept. The performance-based exam tests practical application — understanding why is as important as knowing how. Review the related chapter section for deeper context, diagrams, and hands-on practice drills.

🔍helm lintCatch 80% of errors
📄helm templateLocal render + debug
🧪--dry-runCluster validation
📊helm getInspect deployed state
🔄helm diffSee what will change
⏪rollbackAlways have an escape

🛡️ Helm Debugging Best Practices — Prevention is Better Than Cure

1️⃣
Always lint before deploying

helm lint ./chart --strict is your first line of defense. Run it in CI, run it locally, run it before every commit. A 1-second lint saves 15 minutes of debugging failed deployments.

2️⃣
Use --debug flag liberally

helm install --dry-run --debug and helm template --debug show you exactly what Helm is doing. The debug output includes template rendering steps, value merging, and API calls.

3️⃣
Always use default() in templates

Every .Values reference that MIGHT not exist should have a fallback: {{ .Values.x | default "safe" }}. This single habit prevents 90% of nil pointer panics.

4️⃣
Check K8s events first

When a deployment fails, kubectl get events -n <ns> often reveals the root cause (image pull error, resource quota, admission webhook rejection) faster than any Helm debugging.

5️⃣
Use --atomic for production

Failed upgrades leave releases in a broken state. --atomic auto-rolls back on failure — your safety net. Combined with --wait --timeout 10m, you get a production-grade deployment pattern.

6️⃣
Commit Chart.lock

Dependency resolution is a common source of "it works on my machine" bugs. Chart.lock ensures every CI run and every team member uses identical dependency versions.

Appendix A: Helm CLI Quick Reference

Your go-to command reference for the Helm certification exam and daily work. Every command from install to debugging, organized by function. Print this appendix and keep it beside you during practice.

📋 Reference ⏱️ Quick Lookup 🎯 Exam Essential

A.1 Install, Upgrade, Uninstall & Rollback

CommandPurposeKey Flags
helm install <name> <chart>Install a chart as a new release--namespace, --create-namespace, --atomic, --wait, --timeout, --dry-run, --set, -f, --history-max
helm upgrade <name> <chart>Upgrade an existing release--install, --atomic, --wait, --reuse-values, --reset-values, --dry-run, --set, -f
helm rollback <name> [revision]Rollback to a previous revision--namespace, --wait, --timeout
helm uninstall <name>Uninstall a release--namespace, --keep-history, --dry-run
helm upgrade --install <name> <chart>Install OR upgrade (idempotent)Same as install + upgrade

A.2 Chart Management

CommandPurpose
helm create <name>Scaffold a new chart with default templates
helm lint <chart-path>Validate chart syntax and best practices. Use --strict for rigorous checks
helm package <chart-path>Package chart into a .tgz archive. Use --sign --key <key> for GPG signing
helm dependency update <chart-path>Download dependencies declared in Chart.yaml, update Chart.lock
helm dependency build <chart-path>Rebuild charts/ from Chart.lock (no network, faster)
helm dependency list <chart-path>List all declared dependencies and their resolved versions
helm show chart <chart>Show Chart.yaml metadata for a local or remote chart
helm show values <chart>Show all configurable values with documentation comments
helm show readme <chart>Show the chart's README documentation
helm show all <chart>Show chart metadata + README + values all at once

A.3 Template Debugging

CommandPurposeWhen to Use

helm install <name> ./chart --dry-run --debugSimulate install against live clusterValidate API versions & webhooks

helm get manifest <release>Show the rendered YAML that was appliedCompare with live state for drift

The Golden CLI Workflow — Exam Day Pattern
  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │  EXAM DAY GOLDEN PATTERN — Every Task Follows This Flow           │
  │                                                                   │
  │  1. CREATE chart (if asked):                                      │
  │     helm create <name>                                           │
  │                                                                   │
  │  2. LINT first:                                                   │
  │     helm lint ./chart --strict                                    │
  │     → Fix any errors BEFORE deploying                             │
  │                                                                   │
  │  3. TEMPLATE check:                                               │
  │     helm template test ./chart -f values.yaml --debug             │
  │     → Verify rendered YAML looks correct                          │
  │                                                                   │
  │  4. DRY-RUN (always!):                                            │
  │     helm install <name> ./chart --dry-run --debug                 │
  │     → Validates API versions against live cluster                 │
  │                                                                   │
  │  5. DEPLOY with safety:                                           │
  │     helm upgrade --install <name> ./chart \                       │
  │       -f values.yaml -n <ns> --create-namespace \                 │
  │       --atomic --wait --timeout 5m                                 │
  │                                                                   │
  │  6. VERIFY:                                                       │
  │     helm list -n <ns>                                             │
  │     helm status <name> -n <ns>                                    │
  │                                                                   │
  │  🔑 NEVER skip steps 2-4. A missed lint costs more time           │
  │     than running the lint — every single time.                    │
  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
Q1
What single command creates a chart, installs it to a new namespace, and auto-rolls back on failure?
Answer & Explanation
Answer

helm upgrade --install myapp ./chart -n myns --create-namespace --atomic --wait --timeout 5m

Explanation

This is the "golden command" — installs if new, upgrades if exists, auto-rolls back on failure. --create-namespace creates the namespace if it doesn't exist. This one command covers 90% of exam deploy tasks. 

# Gold Standard Exam Command:
helm upgrade --install anihpj ./chart \
  --namespace prod \
  --create-namespace \
  --values values-prod.yaml \
  --set image.tag=v2.0 \
  --atomic \
  --timeout 5m \
  --wait \
  --debug     # Always add --debug on exam!

# --atomic:    Auto rollback on failure
# --wait:      Wait for all pods ready
# --debug:     Shows rendered templates
# --timeout:   Prevents hanging

Explanation

On the exam, namespace accuracy is critical. Wrong namespace = 0 points. Always read questions carefully for namespace requirements. Use -n <ns> explicitly. Verify with kubectl config view --minify.

Q2
How do you see exactly what YAML Helm would generate without connecting to a cluster?
Answer & Explanation
Answer

helm template myapp ./chart -f values.yaml --debug. This renders YAML locally — no cluster connection needed. Useful for CI/CD and offline validation.

Explanation

This is a key Helm certification concept. The performance-based exam tests practical application — understanding why is as important as knowing how. Review the related chapter section for deeper context, diagrams, and hands-on practice drills.

Q3
A release is stuck in "pending-upgrade" state. What command sequence do you use to investigate?
Answer & Explanation
Answer

helm status <name> -n <ns> → kubectl get jobs -l helm.sh/hook -n <ns> → kubectl logs job/<hook-name>. Hook Jobs are the most common cause of stuck releases — check them first.

Explanation

This is a key Helm certification concept. The performance-based exam tests practical application — understanding why is as important as knowing how. Review the related chapter section for deeper context, diagrams, and hands-on practice drills.

🎯 Exam Pro Tips — CLI Speed

1. Always alias: alias h=helm saves ~2s per command. Over 60 questions, that's 2 minutes saved.
2. Idempotent deploy: helm upgrade --install works whether the release exists or not — use it in every command.
3. Dry-run first: --dry-run --debug catches 80% of errors before they cost you points.
4. Atomic safety net: --atomic --wait --timeout 5m on every install/upgrade for automatic rollback on failure.
5. Namespace check: Always verify kubectl config view --minify | grep namespace BEFORE deploying.

📥installDeploy a new release
🔄upgradeUpdate a release
⏪rollbackGo to previous revision
📦packageCreate .tgz archive
🔍lintValidate chart syntax
📊getInspect releases

Appendix B: Chart.yaml & values.yaml Reference

Complete field reference for Helm chart metadata and configuration files. Every field explained with examples and best practices for the certification exam.

📋 Reference 📐 Schema 🎯 Exam Essential

B.1 Chart.yaml — All Fields Reference

Chart.yaml — Complete Field Reference
apiVersion: v2                    # REQUIRED: Always v2 for Helm 3
name: mychart                     # REQUIRED: Chart name (lowercase, hyphens OK)
version: 1.0.0                    # REQUIRED: Chart version (SemVer 2)
type: application                 # REQUIRED: "application" or "library"

# ── OPTIONAL FIELDS ──
appVersion: "2.1.0"              # Application version (software, not chart)
description: A Helm chart for K8s # Human-readable description
kubeVersion: ">= 1.19.0-0"       # Supported Kubernetes version range
home: https://example.com         # Project home page URL
icon: https://example.com/icon.png # Chart icon URL
sources:                          # Source code URLs
  - https://github.com/org/repo
maintainers:                      # Chart maintainers
  - name: DevOps Team
    email: devops@example.com
    url: https://example.com
keywords:                         # Search keywords for Artifact Hub
  - django
  - web
  - job-board
annotations:                      # Custom metadata
  category: Application
  licenses: MIT
dependencies:                     # Subchart dependencies
  - name: postgresql
    version: "12.x.x"             # SemVer constraint
    repository: https://charts.bitnami.com/bitnami
    condition: postgresql.enabled # Conditional enable/disable
    tags:                         # Group-based toggle
      - database
    import-values:                # Import subchart values
      - child: service.port
        parent: dbPort
deprecated: false                 # Mark chart as deprecated

B.2 values.yaml — Best Practices

📝
Comment Every Key

Every value key should have a YAML comment explaining what it does, its valid range, and its default behavior. Comments serve as inline documentation for anyone using your chart.

🔒
Never Commit Secrets

Placeholder values (empty strings, "changeme") are OK. Real secrets should NEVER be in values.yaml — use --set at deploy time, Sealed Secrets, or External Secrets Operator.

🎯
Sensible Defaults

Your chart should produce a working deployment with helm install myapp ./chart — no extra flags. Defaults should be safe, functional, and production-appropriate.

🏗️
Flat Over Deep

Prefer shallow nesting. image.tag over spec.containers.main.image.tag. Deep nesting makes values harder to override with --set and harder to document.

📦
Group by Resource

Organize values by Kubernetes resource: ingress.*, service.*, resources.*. This matches the mental model of the chart structure and makes values intuitive.

🏷️
Use nameOverride/fullnameOverride

Provide nameOverride and fullnameOverride values so users can customize resource names. Default to chart name if not set.

B.3 Schema Validation Syntax — values.schema.json

values.schema.json — Common Patterns
{
  "$schema": "https://json-schema.org/draft-07/schema#",
  "type": "object",

  "required": ["replicaCount", "image"],

  "properties": {
    "replicaCount": {
      "type": "integer",
      "minimum": 1,
      "maximum": 50,
      "description": "Number of application replicas"
    },
    "image": {
      "type": "object",
      "required": ["repository", "tag"],
      "properties": {
        "repository": {
          "type": "string",
          "minLength": 1,
          "description": "Container image repository"
        },
        "tag": {
          "type": "string",
          "pattern": "^v?\\d+\\.\\d+",
          "description": "Image tag (e.g., v2.1)"
        },
        "pullPolicy": {
          "type": "string",
          "enum": ["Always", "IfNotPresent", "Never"],
          "default": "IfNotPresent"
        }
      }
    },
    "service": {
      "type": "object",
      "properties": {
        "type": {
          "type": "string",
          "enum": ["ClusterIP", "NodePort", "LoadBalancer"]
        },
        "port": {
          "type": "integer",
          "minimum": 1,
          "maximum": 65535
        }
      }
    },
    "ingress": {
      "type": "object",
      "properties": {
        "enabled": { "type": "boolean" },
        "hosts": {
          "type": "array",
          "items": {
            "type": "object",
            "properties": {
              "host": { "type": "string", "format": "hostname" },
              "paths": { "type": "array" }
            }
          }
        }
      }
    }
  },
  "additionalProperties": false
}
📖 JSON Schema Keywords for the Exam

Remember these validators: type (string/integer/boolean/object/array), required (array of mandatory keys), enum (restricted choices), minimum/maximum (number range), minLength/maxLength (string length), pattern (regex), default (fallback value), additionalProperties: false (reject unknown keys).

⚠️ Common Chart.yaml Mistakes on the Exam

1. apiVersion: Always v2 for Helm 3. Using v1 is an instant fail on any chart creation task.
2. version vs appVersion: version tracks the CHART (templates/metadata). appVersion tracks the APPLICATION (image). They are INDEPENDENT — don't confuse them.
3. type field: application for deployable charts, library for reusable template-only charts. A library chart CANNOT be installed.
4. Dependency condition: Must be boolean false, NOT string "false". YAML parses unquoted false as boolean, quoted "false" as string — condition only works with booleans.

Q1
What are the three REQUIRED fields in Chart.yaml?
Answer & Explanation
Answer

apiVersion (always v2), name (chart name), and version (chart SemVer). Those three are mandatory. type is also required in practice but technically defaults to application.

Explanation

The exam may ask you to create a minimal valid Chart.yaml. The absolute minimum is apiVersion, name, and version. However, always include type: application for clarity. Example: apiVersion: v2\nname: myapp\nversion: 0.1.0\ntype: application 

# Minimal valid Chart.yaml:
apiVersion: v2
name: myapp
version: 0.1.0
type: application

# Full-featured example:
apiVersion: v2
name: anihpj
version: 0.2.0
appVersion: "2.1.0"
icon: https://example.com/icon.svg
type: application
maintainers:
  - name: DevOps Team
    email: devops@anihpj.com

Explanation

Chart.yaml requires: apiVersion: v2, name, version. Critical: version (chart) and appVersion (app) are INDEPENDENT. type: application (deployable) or library (template-only).

Q2
Your chart deploys but uses the wrong image tag. How do you check what values were actually applied?
Answer & Explanation
Answer

helm get values <release> --all — shows all computed values merged from values.yaml, -f files, and --set flags. Use it to debug configuration issues.

Explanation

This is a key Helm certification concept. The performance-based exam tests practical application — understanding why is as important as knowing how. Review the related chapter section for deeper context, diagrams, and hands-on practice drills.

Q3
What JSON Schema keyword would you use to restrict a value to specific choices?
Answer & Explanation
Answer

"enum": ["ClusterIP", "NodePort", "LoadBalancer"]. This restricts the value to EXACTLY one of the listed options. Combined with "type": "string" for type safety.

Explanation

values.schema.json validates user values at install/upgrade time. Key keywords: type, required, enum, minimum/maximum, pattern. Catches invalid config before reaching the cluster.

📄Chart.yaml9 required + optional fields
⚙️values.yaml6 best practices
📐SchemaJSON Schema validation
🔒SecretsNever in values.yaml
📝CommentsInline documentation
🎯DefaultsWorking out of the box

Appendix C: Template Functions Cheat Sheet

Rapid reference for all Helm template functions. String manipulation, type conversion, math, dates, flow control, named templates, and pipelines — organized by category for quick lookup during the exam and daily template writing.

📋 Reference 🔧 Functions 🎯 Exam Essential

C.1 String Functions

FunctionExampleResult
quote{{ "hello" | quote }}"hello"
upper{{ "helm" | upper }}HELM
lower{{ "HELM" | lower }}helm
trim{{ " hi " | trim }}hi
replace "old" "new"{{ "v1.0" | replace "." "-" }}v1-0
nospace{{ "h e l m" | nospace }}helm
trunc N{{ "longname" | trunc 5 }}longn
indent N{{ "text" | indent 4 }} text
nindent N{{ "text" | nindent 4 }}\n text (NEWLINE + indent)
contains "sub"{{ contains "helm" "el" }}true

C.2 Type Conversion Functions

FunctionExampleUse Case
toString{{ 42 | toString }}Force integer to string
toYaml{{ .Values.resources | toYaml | nindent 10 }}Convert object to YAML block
fromYaml{{ .Files.Get "c.yaml" | fromYaml }}Parse YAML string to object
toJson{{ .Values.config | toJson }}Convert object to JSON string
fromJson{{ .Files.Get "d.json" | fromJson }}Parse JSON string to object
b64enc{{ .Values.pw | b64enc }}Base64 encode (for Secrets)
b64dec{{ .Files.Get "cert" | b64dec }}Base64 decode
sha256sum{{ "data" | sha256sum }}SHA-256 hash

C.3 Math & Date Functions

CategoryFunctionsExample
Arithmeticadd, sub, mul, div, mod{{ add 2 3 }} → 5
Min/Maxmin, max{{ max 1 5 3 }} → 5
Roundingfloor, ceil, round{{ round 3.7 }} → 4

CategoryFunctionsExample
Current Timenow{{ now | date "2006-01-02" }}
Format Datedate{{ date "2006-01-02" .Release.Time }}
Modify DatedateModify{{ now | dateModify "-24h" }}
ISO 8601htmlDate{{ now | htmlDate }}
📖 Go Date Format

Go uses a reference date for formatting: Mon Jan 2 15:04:05 MST 2006. Remember it as "2006-01-02" for ISO dates. Unlike strftime, Go uses this fixed reference, not placeholder tokens.

C.4 Flow Control — if/else, range, with

Flow Control Quick Reference
  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │  HELM TEMPLATE FLOW CONTROL CHEAT SHEET                          │
  │                                                                   │
  │  IF / ELSE IF / ELSE                                              │
  │  ─────────────────                                                │
  │  {{ if .Values.enabled }}                                         │
  │    # Resource YAML                                                │
  │  {{ else if eq .Values.env "staging" }}                           │
  │    # Alternative YAML                                             │
  │  {{ else }}                                                       │
  │    # Default YAML                                                 │
  │  {{ end }}                                                        │
  │                                                                   │
  │  COMPARISON OPERATORS: eq, ne, lt, gt, le, ge, and, or, not      │
  │  {{ if and .Values.enabled (eq .Values.env "prod") }}             │
  │                                                                   │
  │  RANGE (Iterate Lists)                                            │
  │  ────────────────────                                             │
  │  {{ range .Values.hosts }}                                        │
  │    - host: {{ .host | quote }}    ← . = current item              │
  │  {{ end }}                                                        │
  │                                                                   │
  │  RANGE (Iterate Maps)                                             │
  │  ────────────────────                                             │
  │  {{ range $key, $val := .Values.env }}                            │
  │    {{ $key }}: {{ $val | quote }}                                 │
  │  {{ end }}                                                        │
  │                                                                   │
  │  WITH (Change Scope)                                              │
  │  ──────────────────                                               │
  │  {{ with .Values.db }}        ← . = .Values.db (if non-empty)     │
  │    host: {{ .host }}          ← .host = .Values.db.host           │
  │    port: {{ .port }}                                               │
  │  {{ end }}                                                        │
  │                                                                   │
  │  🔑 ROOT SCOPE: Use $ to access root inside with/range            │
  │  {{ $.Release.Name }}  ← Always works, even in nested blocks      │
  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

C.5 Default Values & Safety Functions

FunctionExampleBehavior
default "val"{{ .Values.x | default "safe" }}Returns fallback if value is nil/empty/false/0
required "msg"{{ required "Image tag required!" .Values.image.tag }}FAILS if value is empty. Use for mandatory keys.
empty{{ if empty .Values.opts }}Returns true if value is nil, "", 0, false, or empty list/map
coalesce{{ coalesce .Values.n .Chart.Name "fallback" }}Returns first non-empty value from list
hasKey{{ if hasKey .Values "db" }}Checks if a key exists in a map (not nil vs exists)
💡 Always Use default()

The single most common Helm error is nil pointer evaluating interface. It happens when you access a .Values key that doesn't exist. The fix: always pipe through default: {{ .Values.optionalKey | default "fallback" }}. Make this a muscle memory habit.

C.6 Named Templates & Pipelines

Named Templates vs include vs template
  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │  NAMED TEMPLATES — DEFINE, INCLUDE, TEMPLATE                     │
  │                                                                   │
  │  DEFINE (in _helpers.tpl)                                        │
  │  ─────────────────────────                                       │
  │  {{- define "mychart.labels" -}}                                 │
  │  app: {{ .Chart.Name }}                                          │
  │  release: {{ .Release.Name }}                                    │
  │  {{- end -}}                                                     │
  │                                                                   │
  │  INCLUDE (USE THIS!) — Returns a string, supports pipelines      │
  │  ────────────────────────────────────────────────────             │
  │  labels:                                                         │
  │  {{ include "mychart.labels" . | nindent 4 }}                    │
  │  ✅ Supports pipelines: | quote, | indent, | nindent              │
  │                                                                   │
  │  TEMPLATE (AVOID!) — Writes inline, no pipeline support           │
  │  ────────────────────────────────────────────────                 │
  │  labels:                                                         │
  │  {{ template "mychart.labels" . }}                               │
  │  ❌ Cannot pipe: | indent, | quote won't work!                   │
  │                                                                   │
  │  PIPELINES — Chain Functions with |                              │
  │  ────────────────────────────────────                             │
  │  {{ .Values.name | upper | quote }}  → "MYAPP"                   │
  │  {{ .Values.config | toYaml | nindent 8 }}                       │
  │  {{ include "mychart.labels" . | indent 4 }}                     │
  │                                                                   │
  │  🔑 ALWAYS use include, NEVER use template.                      │
  │     include = returns string (pipeline-friendly).                 │
  │     template = writes inline (no pipelines).                      │
  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

C.7 Sprig Library — Quick Category Reference

📝
String (18+)

quote, upper, lower, trim, trimAll, trimPrefix, trimSuffix, replace, nospace, trunc, abbrev, abbrevboth, contains, hasPrefix, hasSuffix, repeat, substr, title, untitle, initials, wrap, wrapWith, camelcase, kebabcase, snakecase, swapcase

🔢
Math (12+)

add, sub, mul, div, mod, max, min, floor, ceil, round, add1, sub1

📅
Date (5+)

now, date, dateModify, htmlDate, htmlDateInZone, dateInZone, toDate

📋
Lists (18+)

list, first, last, append, prepend, concat, reverse, uniq, has, slice, sortAlpha, until, untilStep, initial, rest, compact, without, pick, omit, dict

🔤
Type (8+)

toString, toYaml, fromYaml, toJson, fromJson, typeOf, kindOf, kindIs, float64, int64, int

🔐
Encoding (10+)

b64enc, b64dec, sha256sum, sha1sum, md5sum, htpasswd, derivePassword, genPrivateKey, buildCustomCert, uuidv4, randAlphaNum, randAscii, randNumeric

🎯 Template Function Patterns to Memorize for the Exam

1. The Nil-Guard Pattern: {{ .Values.x | default "safe" }} — Piping through default is non-negotiable. Every nullable .Values reference needs this.
2. The Label Pattern: {{ include "mychart.labels" . | nindent 4 }} — Always use include + nindent, never template.
3. The Conditional Resource Pattern: {{ if .Values.ingress.enabled }}...resource YAML...{{ end }} — Wrap entire resources in if blocks.
4. The Quotes Pattern: {{ .Values.name | quote }} — Always quote dynamic strings that may contain YAML special characters (colons, hashes, brackets).
5. The ToYAML Pattern: {{ toYaml .Values.resources | nindent 10 }} — Serialize + indent for embedding complex objects into YAML.

Q1
What is the difference between include and template in Helm templates?
Answer & Explanation
Answer

include returns a string (supports | indent, | quote pipelines). template writes inline (no pipeline support). Always use include.

Explanation

This is a classic exam question. include returns a value that you can pipe through other functions. template writes directly to the output stream — you cannot chain it. If the exam asks you to indent a named template, you MUST use include "name" . | nindent 4. Using template won't work.

Explanation

helm template renders YAML locally without a cluster. Unlike --dry-run, it skips API validation. Use --debug for detailed output. Ideal for CI/CD and offline development.

Q2
How do you prevent nil pointer panics when accessing optional values?
Answer & Explanation
Answer

{{ .Values.optionalKey | default "safeFallback" }}. The default function returns the fallback if the value is nil, empty, false, or zero. This is the single most important safety pattern in Helm templates.

Explanation

"nil pointer evaluating interface" occurs when accessing nested .Values keys where an intermediate key is nil/missing. Fix: {{ .Values.key.subkey | default "fallback" }} or guard with if.

Q3
How do you iterate over a map of key-value pairs in a Helm template?
Answer & Explanation
Answer

{{ range $key, $value := .Values.env }}\n - name: {{ $key }}\n value: {{ $value | quote }}\n{{ end }}. The range block iterates key-value pairs from a map. Use $key and $value as the loop variables.

Explanation

helm template renders YAML locally without a cluster. Unlike --dry-run, it skips API validation. Use --debug for detailed output. Ideal for CI/CD and offline development.

🔢Math12+ functions
📅Date5+ functions

📦Nameddefine/include
🛡️Safetydefault/required

D.1 Chart.yaml — Chart Metadata & Dependencies

Chart.yaml
apiVersion: v2
name: anihpj
description: A Helm chart for the anihpj Django job board application
type: application
version: 0.1.0
appVersion: "2.1.0"
kubeVersion: ">= 1.19.0-0"
home: https://anihpj.example.com
sources:
  - https://github.com/anihpj/anihpj
maintainers:
  - name: DevOps Team
    email: devops@anihpj.com
keywords:
  - django
  - job-board
  - python
dependencies:
  - name: postgresql
    version: "12.x.x"
    repository: https://charts.bitnami.com/bitnami
    condition: postgresql.enabled
  - name: redis
    version: "~18.0.0"
    repository: https://charts.bitnami.com/bitnami
    condition: redis.enabled

D.2 values.yaml — Dev Environment Defaults

values.yaml (Dev)
# ── Deployment Settings ──
replicaCount: 1
image:
  repository: anihpj/anihpj
  pullPolicy: IfNotPresent
  tag: "latest"
nameOverride: ""
fullnameOverride: ""

# ── Service ──
service:
  type: ClusterIP
  port: 8000

# ── Ingress ──
ingress:
  enabled: false
  hosts:
    - host: anihpj.local
      paths:
        - path: /
          pathType: Prefix

# ── Resources ──
resources: {}
  # limits:
  #   cpu: 500m
  #   memory: 256Mi

# ── Auto-scaling ──
autoscaling:
  enabled: false

# ── App Configuration ──
config:
  debug: "true"
  allowedHosts: "*"
  logLevel: "debug"

# ── Secrets (placeholders only!) ──
secrets:
  secretKey: ""
  dbPassword: ""

# ── Dependencies ──
postgresql:
  enabled: true
  auth:
    database: anihpj_dev
    username: anihpj_user
    password: dev_password
redis:
  enabled: true
  auth:
    password: dev_redis_pass

D.3 values-staging.yaml — Staging Overrides

values-staging.yaml
replicaCount: 2
image:
  tag: "v2.1-rc1"
ingress:
  enabled: true
  hosts:
    - host: staging.anihpj.example.com
      paths:
        - path: /
          pathType: Prefix
resources:
  limits:
    cpu: 500m
    memory: 256Mi
  requests:
    cpu: 250m
    memory: 128Mi

D.4 values-prod.yaml — Production Overrides

values-prod.yaml
replicaCount: 5
image:
  pullPolicy: Always
  tag: "v2.0"
ingress:
  enabled: true
  hosts:
    - host: jobs.anihpj.com
      paths:
        - path: /
          pathType: Prefix
resources:
  limits:
    cpu: "1"
    memory: 512Mi
  requests:
    cpu: 500m
    memory: 256Mi
autoscaling:
  enabled: true
  minReplicas: 3
  maxReplicas: 15
  targetCPUUtilizationPercentage: 70
config:
  debug: "false"
  allowedHosts: "jobs.anihpj.com"
  logLevel: "warn"
postgresql:
  auth:
    database: anihpj_prod

D.5 templates/deployment.yaml — Django/Gunicorn Deployment

templates/deployment.yaml
apiVersion: apps/v1
kind: Deployment
metadata:
  name: {{ include "anihpj.fullname" . }}
  labels: {{ include "anihpj.labels" . | nindent 4 }}
spec:
  replicas: {{ .Values.replicaCount }}
  selector:
    matchLabels: {{ include "anihpj.selectorLabels" . | nindent 6 }}
  template:
    metadata:
      labels: {{ include "anihpj.selectorLabels" . | nindent 8 }}
    spec:
      serviceAccountName: {{ include "anihpj.fullname" . }}
      securityContext:
        runAsNonRoot: true
        runAsUser: 1000
      containers:
      - name: {{ .Chart.Name }}
        image: "{{ .Values.image.repository }}:{{ .Values.image.tag }}"
        imagePullPolicy: {{ .Values.image.pullPolicy }}
        ports:
        - containerPort: {{ .Values.service.port }}
          name: http
        envFrom:
        - configMapRef:
            name: {{ include "anihpj.fullname" . }}-config
        - secretRef:
            name: {{ include "anihpj.fullname" . }}-secret
        resources: {{ toYaml .Values.resources | nindent 10 }}
        livenessProbe:
          httpGet:
            path: /health/
            port: http
          initialDelaySeconds: 30
          periodSeconds: 10
        readinessProbe:
          httpGet:
            path: /health/
            port: http
          initialDelaySeconds: 5
          periodSeconds: 5

D.6 templates/service.yaml — ClusterIP Service

templates/service.yaml
apiVersion: v1
kind: Service
metadata:
  name: {{ include "anihpj.fullname" . }}
  labels: {{ include "anihpj.labels" . | nindent 4 }}
spec:
  type: {{ .Values.service.type }}
  ports:
  - port: {{ .Values.service.port }}
    targetPort: http
    protocol: TCP
    name: http
  selector: {{ include "anihpj.selectorLabels" . | nindent 4 }}

D.7 templates/configmap.yaml — Application Configuration

  name: {{ include "anihpj.fullname" . }}-config
  labels: {{ include "anihpj.labels" . | nindent 4 }}
data:
  DEBUG: {{ .Values.config.debug | quote }}
  ALLOWED_HOSTS: {{ .Values.config.allowedHosts | quote }}
  LOG_LEVEL: {{ .Values.config.logLevel | default "info" | quote }}
  DB_HOST: {{ .Release.Name }}-postgresql

  REDIS_HOST: {{ .Release.Name }}-redis-master
  REDIS_PORT: "6379"

D.8 templates/secret.yaml — Database Credentials

templates/secret.yaml

metadata:
  name: {{ include "anihpj.fullname" . }}-secret
  labels: {{ include "anihpj.labels" . | nindent 4 }}
type: Opaque
data:
  SECRET_KEY: {{ .Values.secrets.secretKey | default "changeme" | b64enc | quote }}
  DB_PASSWORD: {{ .Values.secrets.dbPassword | default "changeme" | b64enc | quote }}

D.9 templates/ingress.yaml — Optional HTTP Routing

templates/ingress.yaml
{{ if .Values.ingress.enabled }}
apiVersion: networking.k8s.io/v1
kind: Ingress
metadata:
  name: {{ include "anihpj.fullname" . }}
  labels: {{ include "anihpj.labels" . | nindent 4 }}
spec:
  rules:
  {{ range .Values.ingress.hosts }}
  - host: {{ .host | quote }}
    http:
      paths:
      {{ range .paths }}
      - path: {{ .path }}
        pathType: {{ .pathType }}
        backend:
          service:
            name: {{ include "anihpj.fullname" $ }}
            port:
              number: {{ $.Values.service.port }}
      {{ end }}
  {{ end }}
{{ end }}

D.10 templates/hpa.yaml — Horizontal Pod Autoscaler

templates/hpa.yaml
{{ if .Values.autoscaling.enabled }}
apiVersion: autoscaling/v2
kind: HorizontalPodAutoscaler
metadata:
  name: {{ include "anihpj.fullname" . }}
  labels: {{ include "anihpj.labels" . | nindent 4 }}
spec:
  scaleTargetRef:
    apiVersion: apps/v1
    kind: Deployment
    name: {{ include "anihpj.fullname" . }}
  minReplicas: {{ .Values.autoscaling.minReplicas }}
  maxReplicas: {{ .Values.autoscaling.maxReplicas }}
  metrics:
  - type: Resource
    resource:
      name: cpu
      target:
        type: Utilization
        averageUtilization: {{ .Values.autoscaling.targetCPUUtilizationPercentage }}
{{ end }}

📦 Namespace: {{ .Release.Namespace }}

{{ if .Values.ingress.enabled }}
🌐 Access your application at:
{{ range .Values.ingress.hosts }}
  http://{{ .host }}
{{ end }}
{{ else }}

  kubectl port-forward svc/{{ include "anihpj.fullname" . }} 8000:{{ .Values.service.port }}
{{ end }}

📝 Run migrations:
  kubectl exec deploy/{{ include "anihpj.fullname" . }} -- python manage.py migrate --noinput

🧪 Run tests: helm test {{ .Release.Name }} -n {{ .Release.Namespace }} --logs

D.12 templates/_helpers.tpl — Reusable Template Helpers

templates/_helpers.tpl
{{/* Expand the name of the chart */}}
{{- define "anihpj.name" -}}
{{- default .Chart.Name .Values.nameOverride | trunc 63 | trimSuffix "-" }}
{{- end }}

{{/* Fully qualified app name */}}
{{- define "anihpj.fullname" -}}
{{- printf "%s-%s" .Release.Name .Chart.Name | trunc 63 | trimSuffix "-" }}
{{- end }}

{{/* Common labels */}}
{{- define "anihpj.labels" -}}
app.kubernetes.io/name: {{ include "anihpj.name" . }}
helm.sh/chart: {{ .Chart.Name }}-{{ .Chart.Version }}
app.kubernetes.io/instance: {{ .Release.Name }}
app.kubernetes.io/version: {{ .Chart.AppVersion | quote }}
app.kubernetes.io/managed-by: {{ .Release.Service }}
app.kubernetes.io/component: django
{{- end }}

{{/* Selector labels */}}
{{- define "anihpj.selectorLabels" -}}
app.kubernetes.io/name: {{ include "anihpj.name" . }}
app.kubernetes.io/instance: {{ .Release.Name }}
{{- end }}

D.13 templates/tests/test-connection.yaml — Connectivity Test

templates/tests/test-connection.yaml
apiVersion: v1
kind: Pod
metadata:
  name: "{{ include "anihpj.fullname" . }}-test-connection"
  labels: {{ include "anihpj.labels" . | nindent 4 }}
  annotations:
    "helm.sh/hook": test
spec:
  containers:
  - name: wget
    image: busybox
    command: ['wget']

    - '{{ include "anihpj.fullname" . }}:{{ .Values.service.port }}/health/'
  restartPolicy: Never

D.14 templates/hooks/db-migrate.yaml — Pre-Upgrade DB Migration

templates/hooks/db-migrate.yaml
apiVersion: batch/v1
kind: Job
metadata:
  name: {{ include "anihpj.fullname" . }}-db-migrate
  labels: {{ include "anihpj.labels" . | nindent 4 }}
  annotations:
    "helm.sh/hook": pre-upgrade,pre-install
    "helm.sh/hook-weight": "5"
    "helm.sh/hook-delete-policy": hook-succeeded,before-hook-creation
spec:
  template:
    spec:
      restartPolicy: Never
      serviceAccountName: {{ include "anihpj.fullname" . }}
      containers:
      - name: migrate
        image: "{{ .Values.image.repository }}:{{ .Values.image.tag }}"
        command: ["python", "manage.py", "migrate", "--noinput"]
        envFrom:
        - configMapRef:
            name: {{ include "anihpj.fullname" . }}-config
        - secretRef:
            name: {{ include "anihpj.fullname" . }}-secret
Q1
How does the anihpj chart connect the Django app to PostgreSQL without hardcoding hostnames?
Answer & Explanation
Answer

Via ConfigMap template values: DB_HOST: {{ .Release.Name }}-postgresql. The release name + dependency chart name creates a predictable, dynamic hostname. This is the standard Helm pattern for service discovery.

Explanation

This is a key Helm certification concept. The performance-based exam tests practical application — understanding why is as important as knowing how. Review the related chapter section for deeper context, diagrams, and hands-on practice drills.

Q2
Why does the Secret template use default "changeme" before b64enc?
Answer & Explanation
Answer

To prevent b64enc from receiving a nil/empty value which would cause a template error. The pattern {{ .Values.secret | default "changeme" | b64enc | quote }} provides a safe fallback that still deploys — real values are injected at deploy time via --set or CI/CD.

Explanation

default is the #1 safety function: {{ .Values.x | default "safe" }}. Prevents nil pointer panics — the most common Helm error. Every nullable value reference needs this.

Q3
How could you deploy anihpj to 3 environments with a single chart?
Answer & Explanation
Answer

helm install anihpj-dev ./anihpj-chart -f values.yaml -n dev, helm install anihpj-stg ./chart -f values-staging.yaml -n staging, helm install anihpj-prod ./chart -f values-prod.yaml -n production. One chart, three values files, three namespaces — this is the multi-environment pattern.

Explanation

This is a key Helm certification concept. The performance-based exam tests practical application — understanding why is as important as knowing how. Review the related chapter section for deeper context, diagrams, and hands-on practice drills.

Appendix E: Common Helm Errors & Solutions — Troubleshooting Field Guide

Every Helm error you're likely to encounter — from nil pointer panics to hook timeouts — organized by category with the diagnosis, root cause, and exact fix. This is your troubleshooting companion for the exam and production.

📋 Reference 🔧 Troubleshooting 🎯 Exam Essential

E.1 Template Errors (8 most common)

Error MessageCauseFix
nil pointer evaluating interface {}.Values.xAccessing a .Values key that doesn't existUse {{ .Values.x | default "safe" }} or {{ required "msg" .Values.x }}
error converting YAML to JSONTemplate output is invalid YAMLRun helm template --debug. Check indentation, missing | quote, wrong nindent
wrong type for value; expected int; got stringType mismatch between template and schemaUse --set (not --set-string) for numbers. Or: {{ int .Values.x }}
function "xxx" not definedUsing a non-existent template functionCheck spelling. All functions are from Go text/template + Sprig. No custom functions available.
unexpected "{" in operandSyntax error in template expressionCheck for missing }} or mismatched {{ }} delimiters
template: <name>:<line>: executing... at <.Values.x>: can't evaluate field x in type interface {}Accessing a nested field on nilGuard with {{ if .Values.x }}{{ .Values.x.y }}{{ end }} or use default dict
wrong number of args for <function>: want X got YIncorrect number of arguments to a functionCheck function signature. E.g., replace needs 3 args: replace "old" "new" .Values.str
executing... at <include "name">: error calling include: template "name" not definedNamed template doesn't existVerify the define block exists in _helpers.tpl. Check spelling matches exactly.

E.2 Install/Upgrade Errors (6 common)

Error MessageCauseFix
Error: INSTALLATION FAILED: rendered manifests contain a resource that already existsRelease name conflict — a resource with that name already existsUse a different release name, or helm uninstall <old> first, or check if the resource was created outside Helm
Error: UPGRADE FAILED: <release> has no deployed releasesUpgrading a release that doesn't existUse helm upgrade --install instead, or run helm install first
Error: another operation (install/upgrade/rollback) is in progressPrevious Helm operation didn't completeCheck helm history <release>. Wait for pending operation to finish, or rollback/delete the pending release
timed out waiting for the conditionPods didn't become ready within --timeoutIncrease --timeout 10m. Check pod status: kubectl get pods -n <ns>, kubectl describe pod <name>
Error: create: failed to create: Secret "sh.helm.release.v1.<name>.v<N>" is invalid: data: Too long: must have at most 1048576 bytesRendered manifest exceeds 1MB Secret limitSplit chart into subcharts, use SQL storage driver, or reduce manifest size
no matches for kind "<Kind>" in version "<group/version>"Kubernetes API version not availableUse .Capabilities.APIVersions.Has to check. Upgrade cluster or use older API version

E.3 Dependency Errors (3 common)

Error MessageCauseFix
Error: found in Chart.yaml, but missing in charts/ directory: <name>Dependencies not downloadedRun helm dependency update to download dependency charts
Error: no chart version found for <name>-<version>Version constraint can't be satisfiedCheck the repository has the requested version. Run helm repo update. Adjust version constraint in Chart.yaml
Error: can't get a valid version for repositories <name>. Try changing the version constraintRepository unreachable or version mismatchVerify repository URL, network connectivity, and version constraint syntax (use helm search repo <name> --versions to see available versions)

E.4 Hook & Test Errors (3 common)

Error MessageCauseFix
Error: timed out waiting for the condition (during hook execution)Hook Job never completedCheck hook Job: kubectl get jobs -l helm.sh/hook. View logs: kubectl logs job/<name>. Common: wrong image, DB unreachable, missing env vars
Error: 1 error occurred: <pod>: pods "<name>" is forbidden: unable to validate against any security context constraintHook pod violates security policySet appropriate securityContext in hook template. Check PodSecurityPolicy or SCC constraints
Error: unable to upgrade because hooks are still runningPrevious hook still executingWait for hooks to finish. Delete stuck hook Jobs if necessary: kubectl delete job -l helm.sh/hook
Q1
You see nil pointer evaluating interface {}.Values.db.host. The values.yaml has db: but no host key. What's the fix?
Answer & Explanation
Answer

Either add the missing key to values.yaml, or guard the access: {{ .Values.db.host | default "localhost" }}. The default function catches nil values at any nesting depth.

Explanation

values.yaml provides defaults accessed via .Values. Best practices: comment keys, flat nesting, sensible defaults, never store secrets. Override with --set (highest) or -f files.

Q2
A release upgrade times out. kubectl get pods shows ImagePullBackOff. What happened?
Answer & Explanation
Answer

The image tag in the new chart version doesn't exist or the registry is unreachable. Verify: helm get values <release> --all | grep image. Check registry credentials. Rollback first: helm rollback <release>, then fix the image tag.

Explanation

This is a key Helm certification concept. The performance-based exam tests practical application — understanding why is as important as knowing how. Review the related chapter section for deeper context, diagrams, and hands-on practice drills.

Q3
You run helm install but get "found in Chart.yaml, but missing in charts/ directory". What do you do?
Answer & Explanation
Answer

Run helm dependency update. This downloads the dependency charts declared in Chart.yaml into charts/ and updates Chart.lock. This is the #1 dependency error — simply forgetting to download deps before installing.

Explanation

helm install creates a new release tracked in Kubernetes Secrets. Key flags: --atomic (auto-rollback), --wait (wait for readiness), --dry-run (validate first). Always dry-run before deploying on the exam.

🔧Template8 errors
📥Install/Upgrade6 errors
🔗Dependencies3 errors
🪝Hooks3 errors
📊Total20 errors cataloged
🎯ExamAll domains covered

Everything you need to know about the Helm certification exam in one place — format, scoring, domain weights, recommended resources, and last-minute tips. Read this the night before your exam.

F.1 Exam Format & Requirements

⏱️
120 Minutes

2 hours of hands-on tasks in a live terminal. ~2 minutes per question. Time management is critical — use the 3-pass strategy from Chapter 15.

💻
Browser-Based Terminal

You get a browser-based terminal with Helm v3, kubectl, and a text editor. No local tools needed. Test your browser compatibility 48 hours before.

🪪
Government ID Required

Must match registration name. Clean workspace — no papers, phones, or secondary screens. Proctor will ask for a room scan.

F.2 Domain Weights — What to Focus On

📦 Helm Core (40%)Charts, Releases, Repos, Values, Install/Upgrade
🔧 Templates (25%)Go Templates, Sprig, Built-in Objects, Functions
🚀 Advanced (20%)Dependencies, Hooks, Testing, Schema Validation
🏭 Production (15%)Repositories, Security, CI/CD, Troubleshooting
💡 Exam Strategy

Focus 50% of your study time on Helm Core (40% weight). Templates are the second highest weight at 25% — master default, include, if/range/with, and the 5 built-in objects. Advanced features (20%) and Production (15%) are lower priority but still testable.

F.3 Recommended Resources

📖
Official Helm Docs

helm.sh/docs — The authoritative source. Focus on: Chart Template Guide, Built-in Objects, and Template Function List.

📦
Artifact Hub

artifacthub.io — Study real charts. Look at how Bitnami, Prometheus, and Grafana structure their charts.

🛠️
Practice Environment

Use minikube or kind for local practice. Deploy real charts, write templates, break things and fix them. Hands-on practice is irreplaceable.

📝
This Guide's Practice Q&As

All 20 chapters in this guide contain 10 practice questions each — 200+ questions total. Chapters 15-17 add another 100 exam-focused questions.

📋
Helm CLI Reference

Appendix A of this guide. Print it out or keep it open in a second tab during practice. Memorize the golden workflow pattern.

🎯
Exam Simulator

Chapter 16, Lab 5: Full 60-minute, 10-task simulation. Complete it under timed conditions at least twice before your exam date.

F.4 Last-Minute Exam Checklist

1
48 Hours Before

Run the exam provider's system compatibility check. Verify: webcam, microphone, internet speed (min 5 Mbps). Ensure your workspace meets requirements (clear desk, no screens).

2
24 Hours Before

Review Appendices A-F (this section). Practice the golden CLI workflow 5 times. Run Lab 5 (full simulation) one more time. Review your personal error list.

3
Morning Of

Eat a good breakfast. Have water nearby (proctor may allow clear bottle). Prepare your ID. Log in 30 minutes early for check-in. Do NOT cram — trust your preparation.

4
First 5 Minutes

Set up aliases: alias h=helm; alias k=kubectl. Enable completion: source <(helm completion bash). Configure vim: set tabstop=2 expandtab. Read all questions first.

5
During the Exam

Use the 3-pass strategy. Flag hard questions — never spend >5 min on one. Always --dry-run before deploying. Use helm create for chart tasks. Check namespaces!

6
Last 10 Minutes

Review flagged questions. Verify namespace correctness on all tasks. Run helm list -A to confirm all expected releases are deployed. Submit with confidence!
